# Rule 30: the statements and the catalog of partial results

This Lake project (package `rule30prize`, library `Ruliology`) holds the official Lean statements of the Rule 30 Prize problems and a catalog of proved partial results about rule 30, all in the namespace `Ruliology.CellularAutomata`. It is plain Lean 4.35.0, without Mathlib.

**Problem 1 is open.** Everything here is a partial result.

- **The statements:** `Ruliology/CellularAutomata/Rule30Jen.lean` defines `rule`, `cell` and `EventuallyPeriodic`. `Ruliology/CellularAutomata/Rule30Prize.lean` defines `center`, `Problem1` and `Problem2`. The kit's [README](../README.md) quotes them.
- **The catalog:** 54 modules. `Ruliology.lean` imports the 40 `Rule30*` modules; their import closure adds the 13 data modules in `Rule30Certificates/` and `BusyBeaverLifetimes`. Each module's docstring records its claim, method and sources.
- **Axioms:** every theorem uses only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. None uses `sorry` or `native_decide`.
- **Provenance:** the 54 catalog modules come from the Ruliology catalog of 2026-10-15, byte for byte except one docstring sentence in `Rule30ZeroBoundaryWidth52`. `SHA256SUMS` lists their hashes as copied, which the kit's first commit, `c2dab06`, matches (`shasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS` in a checkout of it). Since then their comments have dropped catalog numbering and provenance, eight modules changed for Lean 4.35.0, and every file became a module of Lean's module system (`module`, `public import`, `@[expose] public section`, so all definitions stay unfoldable for importers) with deprecated names replaced. `Rule30RightPeriods` has since gained the contributed half-rate bound `per_upper_half` (see [Added in the kit](#added-in-the-kit)), with its earlier theorems unchanged; so have `Rule30BlackRuns`, the self-shielded Theorems B and D, `Rule30ZeroBoundary`, the integer form `Q01_iff_integer`, and `Rule30Ether`, the condition `Lock`. `git log lean/` shows each change.
- **Added in the kit:** modules proved after the snapshot, proved during the attempt but left out of the catalog, or contributed by participants in the prize. [Added in the kit](#added-in-the-kit) lists them, with credits.

## Status by period

| Case | Status | Lean (`lean/`, namespace `Ruliology.CellularAutomata.*`) |
|---|---|---|
| Period 1 | **Proved** for every column, and for every nonzero configuration with a leftmost black cell | `Rule30Prize.no_column_eventually_constant`, `center_not_eventually_period_one`, `Rule30FiniteConfigs.no_column_eventually_constant` |
| Small periods | **Proved:** no eventual period P ≤ 4096 with transient ≤ 8192 | `Rule30Window` |
| Any period | **Proved:** T + P ≥ 16359 | `Rule30FactorWindow` |
| Period 2 | Reduced to Q01, a statement about the zero-boundary left half. Q01 is **open** in general. It is proved in Lean for every start of width ≤ 52. The milestone `CQ01Empty` follows from `Q01Gap`, an **open** statement that `Q01` implies. | `Rule30PeriodTwo`, `Rule30PeriodTwoCoupled`, `Rule30PeriodTwoSideways`, `Rule30ZeroBoundary*`, `Rule30ZeroBoundaryWidth52`, `Rule30PeriodTwoWidth5/6`, `Rule30GateTrap` |
| Least period 3 | Reduced. 001 needs one of Q001 or C3s001; the third route, AbsorptionT t001, is **false** (`Rule30EtherThreeNotAbsorption`). 011 needs C3w(011). The three are **open**. The ether-and-wall route is closed: the 12 × 3 ether has no wall of any width. | `Rule30EtherThree*` |
| Least period 4 | Reduced to Absorption, the ether-and-wall trap, which is **open**, and to the weaker `Lock` (**open**): column 1 of every finite right half driven by the word is eventually periodic at the white times, or some column is eventually periodic. The pure ether lies in no trap of width ≤ 20. Partial: single-cell and high-support absorption are proved (`Rule30EtherSingleCell`). | `Rule30Ether`, `Rule30PeriodFourSkeleton`, `Rule30EtherTrapWidth20`, `Rule30EtherSingleCell` |
| Longer periods | **Open.** Some words are excluded, for example by long black runs, 30 certified words, every `1 0^q` with `q ≥ 9`, and arbitrary mixtures with all white runs at least 17. | `Rule30BlackRuns`, `Rule30BlackRunBlocks`, `Rule30StripCertificateTable`, `Rule30WhiteRunEight`, `Rule30WhiteEnd`, `Rule30WhiteEndNine`, `Rule30White17Runs`, `Rule30GrammarRuns` |

Barriers are also proved:
- Rules 90, 150 and 210 share every structural fact the argument uses, yet their center columns are eventually constant (`Rule30Realization`).
- Local, strip, finite-state, additive and edge-DFA invariants cannot prove Q01 (`Rule30ZeroBoundaryBarriers`).
- The finite left half cannot be replaced by a periodic one: with the ether on the left and a finite right half, the center column is any of the three words of least period 4, or the alternating word, from time 0 (`Rule30EtherWallWitness`).

## Building

You need [elan](https://github.com/leanprover/elan); it installs Lean 4.35.0 from `lean-toolchain`.

`lake build` builds everything, but a cold build runs modules in parallel. The four `Rule30Certificates/ZeroBoundaryWidth52Chunks*` modules take about 20 s and 3 GB each, so a cold parallel build can need about 12.5 GB before the contributed tail modules below. Build these heavy data modules sequentially too.

Building one module at a time is safest; each `lake build` below compiles exactly one module, and peak memory stays under 4 GB.

```sh
cd lean
for m in Rule30Predecessors Rule30PredecessorCounts BusyBeaverLifetimes Rule30Blocks Rule30Jen \
    Rule30Prize Rule30Window Rule30MaskedJen Rule30Diagonals Rule30BlackRuns Rule30BlackRunBlocks \
    Rule30StripCertificates Rule30StripCertificateTable Rule30DrivenLeft Rule30RightPeriods \
    Rule30WhiteSeed Rule30Realization Rule30FactorWindow Rule30PeriodTwo Rule30ZeroBoundary \
    Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripe Rule30ZeroBoundarySmall Rule30ZeroBoundaryBarriers Rule30Ether \
    Rule30PeriodTwoCoupled Rule30PeriodFourSkeleton Rule30ZeroBoundaryLanes \
    Rule30Certificates.ZeroBoundaryWidth52Chunks{0,1,2,3} Rule30ZeroBoundaryWidth52 \
    Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFront Rule30Certificates.ZeroBoundaryStripeCycles{0,1,2,3} \
    Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFamily Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFour \
    Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFrontLift Rule30Certificates.StripeTail13Completion \
    Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFourComplete Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeStructure Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripePrimitiveRoots Rule30EtherTrap Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeCascade \
    Rule30Certificates.EtherTrap{0,1,2,3,4,5} \
    Rule30EtherSingleCellAux Rule30EtherSingleCellCases Rule30EtherSingleCell \
    Rule30EtherTrapWidth20 Rule30PeriodTwoLanguage Rule30PeriodTwoWidth5 Rule30PeriodTwoWidth6 \
    Rule30EtherThree Rule30EtherThreeNoWall Rule30EtherThreeTrap Rule30EtherThreeReach \
    Rule30EtherThreeLeft Rule30EtherThreeSkeleton Rule30WhiteRunEight Rule30PeriodTwoSideways \
    Rule30LightCone Rule30FiniteConfigs Rule30FiniteMaskedJen Rule30Coupled Rule30FiniteBlackRuns Rule30FiniteBlackRunBlocks Rule30FiniteDiagonals Rule30AgedLeftDiagonals Rule30ArbitraryWhitePadding Rule30WhitePaddingMixtures Rule30FiniteWhiteRuns Rule30ClockBirthDemodulation \
    Rule30Certificates.WhiteEndChecks Rule30WhiteEnd Rule30WhiteEndNine \
    Rule30WhitePadding \
    Rule30FiniteStrips \
    Rule30WhitePulse \
    Rule30ZeroPredecessor \
    Rule30BackwardTube \
    Rule30WhitePaddingVacuumCriterion \
    Rule30BackwardVacuum \
    Rule30SparsePreimage \
    Rule30Certificates.FiveTailTube \
    Rule30Certificates.SixTailBackward \
    Rule30RestrictedBackwardTube \
    Rule30Certificates.SixTailEndpoints \
    Rule30Certificates.WhitePaddingFamilies \
    Rule30UniformWhitePadding \
    Rule30WordRotation \
    Rule30ShortTail \
    Rule30FiveTailReduction \
    Rule30VacuumExample \
    Rule30WhitePadding10101 \
    Rule30FiveTail \
    Rule30ForbiddenSuffix \
    Rule30SixTailReduction \
    Rule30SixTail \
    Rule30SixTailSharpened \
    Rule30SuffixFamilies \
    Rule30Certificates.StripeMeeting \
    Rule30StripeMeeting \
    Rule30BlackRunErosion \
    Rule30LeftReset \
    Rule30LeftUnbounded \
    Rule30SettledBarrier \
    Rule30FrontLaw \
    Rule30SettledBarrierEffective \
    Rule30WhiteSpacing \
    Rule30RunEnvelopes Rule30FastImage Rule30LongWhiteSupport Rule30RankedWords \
    Rule30Certificates.White17 Rule30White17Runs Rule30Certificates.Grammar \
    Rule30GrammarRuns Rule30ParametricStripCertificates Rule30PaddedWords Rule30CoupledEmptyRight \
    Rule30Certificates.ResetMixtures Rule30ResetMixtures Rule30CenterCylinders \
    Rule30BlackTimeGrowth Rule30ConeCube Rule30SourceEnergy Rule30BlackPhaseEscape \
    Rule30Certificates.PeriodTwoMass Rule30PeriodTwoMass \
    Rule30PeriodTwoNoFloor Rule30PeriodTwoLadder \
    Rule30Certificates.PeriodTwoWidth13 Rule30PeriodTwoWidth13; do
  lake build Ruliology.CellularAutomata.$m || break
done
lake build
```

On 2026-10-15 this took 285 s in total on an Apple M3 Max with 48 GB; the four upstream modules added on 2026-10-23 take about 45 s more. The contributed white-padding modules are measured separately below. Every module not listed below built in under 7 s and 1.2 GB.

| Module | Time | Peak memory |
|---|---|---|
| `Rule30Certificates/ZeroBoundaryWidth52Chunks0`–`3` | 22–24 s each | 2.7–2.8 GB each |
| `Rule30StripCertificateTable` | 21 s | 3.9 GB |
| `Rule30BlackRunBlocks` | 20 s | 2.3 GB |
| `Rule30ZeroBoundarySmall` | 19 s | 3.2 GB |
| `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripe` | 18 s | 1.8 GB |
| `Rule30Certificates/WhiteEndChecks` | 34 s | 0.8 GB |
| `Rule30Certificates/PeriodTwoWidth13` | 40 s | 0.7 GB |
| `Rule30Certificates/White17` | 21.604 s (2026-10-28, clean module with built imports) | 845,365,248 bytes |
| `Rule30Certificates/Grammar` | 27.791 s (2026-10-28, clean module with built imports) | 845,393,920 bytes |
| `Rule30Certificates/ResetMixtures` | 2.864 s (2026-10-28, clean module with built imports) | 416,743,424 bytes |
| `Rule30Certificates/StripeMeeting` | 47 s | 5.5 GB |

The White17 and Grammar rows were remeasured on Linux x86-64 with Lean 4.35.0 on 2026-10-28, each compiled alone with built imports. Their elapsed times cover the recorded command; memory is maximum resident set size from `/usr/bin/time -v`, converted from KiB to bytes.

To check axioms, compile a file outside this folder from here:

```sh
cat > /tmp/Axioms.lean <<'EOF'
import Ruliology
#print axioms Ruliology.CellularAutomata.Rule30Prize.center_not_eventually_period_one
EOF
lake env lean /tmp/Axioms.lean
```

## Added in the kit

These modules are not in the 2026-10-15 snapshot of the catalog, and `SHA256SUMS` does not list them. All use only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. To add one, see [Contributing a partial result](../README.md#contributing-a-partial-result).

| Module | Added | What it proves | Headline theorems |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rule30DrivenLeftWindows` | 2026-11-10 | For every positive driver period `P`, every start with leftmost black cell at `-m` (`m > 0`), and every onset `T`, a period-`P` agreement window of length `N` in driven column `-1` satisfies `N + 1 ≤ T + P + m`; consequently a disagreement occurs before `2 * T + P + m`. A finite-window helper propagates agreement leftward using only driver agreement on that window. Quantitative corollaries of inverse-rule reconstruction and the moving left edge; no center-column period is excluded. Contributed by David Condrey with OpenAI Codex. | `window_columns_periodic`, `column_neg_one_period_window_bound`, `exists_period_disagreement_before` |
| `Rule30LocalFactor` | 2026-11-10 | For every width `n`, codomain `Y : Type`, observable `g : Word n → Y` and update `H : Y → Y`, if `g (step z) = H (g (middle z))` for every input block of length `n + 2`, then `g` is constant. Uses the official Rule 30 local rule and left permutivity; no algebraic or finiteness assumption on the codomain or update. The universal-block premise is essential; no obstruction restricted to the singleton orbit is proved. Contributed by David Condrey with OpenAI Codex. | `autonomous_is_constant` |
| `Rule30SourceObservables` | 2026-11-10 | For arbitrary neighboring Rule 30 column streams and every horizon, `3*C(T) ≤ T + 5*A(T) + 3`, where `A` counts the run-end source `c && !r`; source silence gives `3*C(T) ≤ T+3`. Includes every time interval of every official singleton column. Separately, eight consecutive observations of three adjacent run-end sources determine the four-cell state at time one, with the exterior-cell evolution constraint retained. No unconditional asymptotic source frequency or eventual-nonperiodicity conclusion. Contributed by David Condrey with OpenAI Codex. | `density_bound`, `quiet_bound`, `cell_density_bound`, `Band.recover` |
| `Rule30MovingWaves`, `Rule30MovingStrip`, `Rule30Certificates.MovingReturn01`–`11` | 2026-11-08 | Minimum return period 12 for positive spatial entropy / exponential spatial language growth in the rightward light-speed frame `Phi x i = x i xor (x (i+1) \|\| x (i+2))`. For every return period `1 ≤ P ≤ 11` and every word length `m`, all spatial words of length `m` belong to an explicit certified list of size at most `81 + 14 * m`, giving linear growth and zero spatial entropy. At period 12, two explicit 34-cell tiles encode arbitrary bi-infinite binary messages with exact minimal moving return period 12, yielding an injective embedding of all `(n / 34)`-bit binary words into length-`n` spatial words. 12 is the least positive return with exponential spatial word growth. Contributed by Emerson Villafuerte; Codex (OpenAI), Antigravity (Google DeepMind) as Rule 30 Prize submission [`R30-20261108-9933fc`](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261108-9933fc). | `low_period_linear_words`, `free_binary_period_twelve`, `exponential_marked_rings`, `exponential_words_twelve`, `minimum_exponential_period_twelve` |
| `Rule30TwoBlockCounts` | 2026-11-06 | The four 2-block transition counts (`count00`, `count01`, `count10`, `count11`) of a Boolean sequence `f` and their exact identities on the rule-30 center column. For every `n`, the counts partition `[0, n)` (`block_counts_sum`), `blackCount n = count10 center n + count11 center n` (`blackCount_step_decomposition`), `count10 center n - count01 center n = 1 - bit center n` (`transition_balance_10_01`), `2 · blackCount n - n = (count11 center n - count00 center n) + (1 - bit center n)` (`discrepancy_eq_homogeneous_block_diff`), and `(count01 center n + count10 center n) - (count11 center n + count00 center n) = 4 · count10 center n - n + 2 · (bit center n - 1)` (`heterogeneous_balance_eq_four_count10`). By the rule-30 update, each center transition `(center t, center (t + 1))` is read off the three middle cells `-1, 0, 1` of row `t` (`center_block_11_eq_left_white`, `center_block_10_eq_left_black`, `center_block_01_eq_neighbors_diff`, `center_block_00_eq_white_symmetric`), giving the single-row three-column identity `2 · blackCount n - n = (spatialBlackLeftWhiteCount n - spatialWhiteSymmetricCount n) + (1 - bit center n)` (`discrepancy_eq_spatial_three_column_diff`). Hence `Problem2` holds iff `(count11 center n - count00 center n).natAbs = o(n)` (`problem2_iff_block_imbalance_sublinear`), and iff `(spatialBlackLeftWhiteCount n - spatialWhiteSymmetricCount n).natAbs = o(n)` (`problem2_iff_spatial_three_column_sublinear`). Contributed by Matthew Payton Ulrey with Antigravity (Google DeepMind) as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261106-2b2509](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261106-2b2509). | `discrepancy_eq_homogeneous_block_diff`, `heterogeneous_balance_eq_four_count10`, `discrepancy_eq_spatial_three_column_diff`, `problem2_iff_block_imbalance_sublinear`, `problem2_iff_spatial_three_column_sublinear`, `center_block_11_eq_left_white`, `center_block_10_eq_left_black`, `center_block_01_eq_neighbors_diff`, `center_block_00_eq_white_symmetric`, `block_counts_sum`, `blackCount_step_decomposition`, `transition_balance_10_01` |
| `Rule30HealingBound` | 2026-11-03 | The universal healing bound. For every rule-30 evolution `E` of the whole line (any background orbit, periodic or not) and every evolution `G` agreeing with `E` at time 0 on `x ≤ -1`, put `K 0 = -1` and `K (t + 1) = K t + 1` exactly when `E t (K t - t)` is black. Then `G = E` on every diagonal `x + t ≤ K t`: a black newest known cell masks its unknown right neighbour, so the next diagonal is recruited. Hence the leftmost disagreement lies at `x ≥ K t - t + 1`, and damage moves left no faster than the frontier; where its climb rate has a mean (as for periodic backgrounds) the speed is at most one minus it. Masking itself is classical (`Rule30MaskedJen.left_of_black`); the recurrence and the all-tail agreement below it are new. Nothing here concerns the centre column. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI); cumulative recruitment counting (`recruitCount`, `K_eq_sub_one_add_recruitCount`, `K_eq_neg_one_of_all_white`, `K_eq_sub_one_of_all_black`), arbitrary-anchor healing bounds (`KFrom`, `healing_bound_from`, `front_ge_from`), and stationary black-column shielding (`stationary_wall_of_col_black`) by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261104-7c7878](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261104-7c7878), and `KFrom` envelope bounds, perturbation invariance (`KFrom G a t = KFrom E a t`), zero-recruitment equivalence (`KFrom E a t = a`), and boundary-twin sharpness ([R30-20261106-09b377](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261106-09b377)). | `healing_bound`, `front_ge`, `K_succ_bounds`, `K_add_bounds`, `K_bounds`, `front_pos_nonincreasing`, `recruitCount`, `K_eq_sub_one_add_recruitCount`, `K_eq_neg_one_of_all_white`, `K_eq_sub_one_of_all_black`, `KFrom`, `KFrom_neg_one_eq_K`, `healing_bound_from`, `front_ge_from`, `KFrom_eq_add_of_col_black`, `stationary_wall_of_col_black`, `KFrom_succ_bounds`, `KFrom_bounds`, `KFrom_eq_of_init_agree`, `K_eq_of_init_agree`, `KFrom_eq_anchor_iff_all_white`, `twin_flip_at_front_iff_KFrom_eq_anchor` |
| `Rule30ErrorEdge` | 2026-11-05 | The error edge of two arrays with the left-diagonal recurrence, the lowest wheel on which they differ. Agreement below it is kept, so it never retreats (`edge_le`). At an edge of index at least 1 the arrays agree there one tick later exactly when its parent is black (`heal_iff`); the difference may disappear entirely. Black-parent absorption is the recruiting step of `Rule30HealingBound`; white-parent memory appears inside `Rule30UniversalLeftSide.child_white`; the exact interior Boolean criterion is `Rule30LightCone.rule_flip_right_iff`. After a heal at an old edge of index at least 2, the first wheel above it is white if it agrees and the second is black if both agree, so the jump constrains the new parent: white after a jump of 2, black after 3, black one tick later after 4, and for a white new parent black one tick later after 5 and white one tick later after 6 or 7. The proofs decide Boolean identities on a window of at most nine wheels. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI). | `agree_step`, `agree_after`, `edge_le`, `heal_iff`, `edge_stall`, `edge_heal`, `white_above`, `black_two_above`, `jump_two`, `jump_three`, `jump_four`, `jump_five`, `jump_six`, `jump_seven` |
| `Rule30EtherEdgeZero`, `Rule30RootedFront` | 2026-11-03 | The classic ether of `Rule30Ether` absorbs an inserted black edge: at each of its 28 alignments, after a black cell with white to its right is inserted, from a finite time `t1 ≤ 222` on the ether is undisturbed at and left of a fixed B3 column `xB` and some cell of the next 13 always differs, so the leftmost disturbance's speed is exactly zero (`edge_pinned`). The trap `trap_invariant` is proved on the whole line (`trap_two_sided`), and the kernel checks each entry. The same holds for every row agreeing with the inserted edge's on `x ≤ 198` (`edge_pinned_tail`) and, by a rooted-front observer, on `x ≤ 87` (`edge_pinned_tail87`): the observer follows the leftmost difference with three-valued knowledge of the next two cells, is sound for any two whole-line evolutions (`check_sound`), and is run by the kernel on bit-mask rows. `87` is least for exact agreement with the reference at these entries; nothing here concerns eventual pinning in general, the centre column or the Prize problems. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI). | `edge_pinned`, `edge_pinned_tail`, `trap_two_sided`, `wall_ne_ref_all`, `edge_pinned_tail87`, `check_sound`, `tick_sound` |
| `Rule30ClosingLaw` | 2026-11-03 | The closing law of prefix doublings on gate clocks (`Rule30GateClocks`), for every gate whose derivative in its newer input depends on the older one (OR included, `quadratic_or`). For a pair "double, keep" with the periods it carries, `order_countdown_gate`'s inequality is an equality: the difference order drops by exactly one (`order_countdown_exact`; the guard `order s M + 1 < M` excludes the capped order of a constant stream). Hence, after a doubling at period `q0 = 2 ^ E`, `m` pairs `10` and a doubling, `m + 1 ≤ q0`, and the next column doubles iff `m + 1 = q0` (`closing_law`, `closing_law_or`): closing runs have length one less than a power of two, `1 (10)^2 1 1` never occurs (`no_1101011`), and once `q0 ≥ 4` five consecutive columns hold at most three doublings (`five_letter`). The OR case of `11011` at `q0 ≥ 4` is already in kit pull request 55. Nothing here concerns the centre column. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI); multi-block `5k`-column prefix-exponent bounds and scaled `3/5`-slope inequalities (`prefixExp_mono`, `five_letter_mul`, `prefixExp_add_five_div_le`, `five_mul_prefixExp_sub_le`) and Rule 30 right-edge OR gate specializations (`order_countdown_exact_or`, `no_three_at_or`, `no_11011_at_or`, `five_letter_mul_or`, `five_mul_prefixExp_sub_le_or`) by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261104-6f3186](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261104-6f3186), and order-zero non-closure, cumulative `(10)^m` countdown exponents, complementary keep characterization (`m + 1 < 2 ^ E`), and the `E + 2 ^ E + 2` closing-horizon jump ([R30-20261106-8e3d0b](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261106-8e3d0b)). | `order_countdown_exact`, `closing_law`, `closing_law_or`, `no_1101011`, `five_letter`, `prefixExp_mono`, `prefixExp_add_le`, `five_letter_mul`, `prefixExp_add_five_div_le`, `five_mul_prefixExp_sub_le`, `order_countdown_exact_or`, `no_three_at_or`, `no_11011_at_or`, `five_letter_or`, `five_letter_mul_or`, `five_mul_prefixExp_sub_le_or`, `not_period_two_mul_of_order_zero`, `not_period_two_mul_of_order_zero_or`, `prefixExp_after_closing_pairs`, `closing_law_keep_iff_lt`, `closing_law_keep_iff_lt_or`, `prefixExp_at_closing_horizon`, `prefixExp_at_closing_horizon_or` |
| `Rule30MovingWall` | 2026-11-03 | Moving walls: exact front speeds of an inserted edge on two ring backgrounds. Insert one black cell, white to its right, into the background orbit of the ring word `100100000` (ring orbit of period 57, alignment (0, 0)) or `1010000` (ring orbit of period 63, alignment (0, 4)): for every `t ≥ 300` an anchor `A` has the background intact at and left of it, a difference within 24 (16) cells right of it, and `-14 ≤ 19 (A + 72) + 5 (t - 300) ≤ 22` (`-20 ≤ 9 (A + 55) + 2 (t - 300) ≤ 4`), so the front moves left at exactly `5/19` (`2/9`) (`Ring57.edge_law`, `Ring63.edge_law`); the same laws hold for every rule-30 evolution agreeing with the inserted edge at time 0 on `x ≤ 252` (`x ≤ 261`), whatever its cells beyond (`edge_law_tail`). The proof is a closed set of anchored window states (498 and 74) with a moving anchor, checked by the kernel on bit masks, with an exact potential; `step_sound` makes one tick sound on every whole-line evolution whatever the cell beyond the window. The method is `Rule30EtherThreeErosion`'s finite front state with a potential, here an exact identity with a moving anchor; `Rule30Ether`'s trap is the stationary case. The edge map is the gate clock with OR, so bits `0 … k + 1` of every code have period dividing `2 ^ B (k + 2)` (`Rule30GateClocks.prefixExp_le_B`), and an episode of `horizon k p = lcm (2 ^ B (k + 2), p)` ticks lasts for ever (`episode_extend`) and gives an eternal point by pasting `E` beyond depth `k + 1` (`eternal_of_long`). | `Rule30GateTrapLift` | 2026-11-09 | The gate trap at eighteen columns. The sixteen-column graph of `Rule30GateTrap`'s trap reads the gap word `3535353` (`trap_reads_3535353`), so excluding it requires information beyond that free-boundary graph. An 18-column window whose first sixteen cells are a window of `trap` stays such a window under the double step, with column `19` free (`win18_step2`), and in that lift no path reads `3535353`: after the window of a right half `R` enters the trap at an odd time, `u R` never reads `3, 5, 3, 5, 3, 5, 3` (`no_3535353_of_entry`). The word is minimal for the lift (`lift_minimal_paths`). The method is that of `Rule30GateTrapWords`: the path sets of the word in the lift, from every black lift window, as data, one kernel check per sample and per chunk of at most 192 windows (`wordCheck18`, `not_reads18_of_check`). `Q01GapCore` (**open**) is `Q01GapWords` with `3535353` excluded as well: `Q01GapWords` implies it (`Q01GapCore_of_Q01GapWords`), and it still gives `¬ FinSupp (Psi R)` for every right half whose window enters the trap (`not_finSupp_Psi_of_Q01GapCore`), hence `CQ01Empty` (`CQ01Empty_of_Q01GapCore`). The external R30-20261030-60990f excludes `33`, `3553` and `3535353` at every index for the trajectories of its invariant pool of 18-cell windows, among them the one-cell right seed; here the premise is entry into the kit's whole trap. | `Rule30PeriodThreeLanguage` | 2026-11-08 | Period 3: the language of the bad class of the right strip, and the weaker left statements it gives. `occurs3` and `no_pattern3` extend the subset simulation of `Rule30PeriodTwoLanguage` to period 3, three ticks per letter: if a kernel check shows that no path inside the bad class of a bad-class certificate reads a word in column `1` at a fixed phase of consecutive periods, then column `1` eventually never reads it. Two certificates apply it. Beside an eventual `011` center (strip width `7`), column `1` at phase `0` never reads `00`, `011110` or six longer words (`col1_011`); beside `001` (width `10`), column `1` at phase `1` has black runs of length `2` to `4` and white runs of length `2` to `5`, and avoids six longer words (`col1_001`). Column `-1` is the complement there (`free_bits_011`, `free_bits_001`). So `C3w w011` can be weakened to `C3r011`, which only has to exclude the finitely supported left configurations whose free bits also avoid the complements (`11`, `100001`, ...), and `C3s001` to `C3g001`. Each still excludes its tail of the center column (`not_eventually_011_of_C3r`, `not_eventually_001_of_C3g`), and together they exclude an eventually `3`-periodic center (`no_eventual_period_three_of_C3g`). `C3r011` and `C3g001` are open. The lower bounds beside `001` (no `010`, no `101`) are also in the external `lhallee/rule30-open`'s `ThreeRuns`, for every right half, and its `ConstrainedLeft001` reduces `001` with that language. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261108-261a30](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261108-261a30). | `no_pattern3`, `col1_011`, `free_bits_011`, `col1_001`, `free_bits_001`, `C3r011_of_C3w`, `not_eventually_011_of_C3r`, `C3g001_of_C3s`, `not_eventually_001_of_C3g`, `no_eventual_period_three_of_C3g` |
| `Rule30BlackRuns` (self-shielded Theorems B and D) | 2026-10-31 | The pattern `(white black)^j` that Lemma A* forces after a black run of length `9 j` moves two columns right every two steps without reading the cell on its right, so column `1` is determined for `4 j` steps instead of the `2 j` of the light cone; with masked Jen, which needs no agreement while the center is black, this halves the slope of Theorems B and D, for every period and every run. A black run of length `r < P` followed by a white cell is excluded once `9 ⌈(P - r) / 4⌉ ≤ r` (Theorem B: `9 ⌈(P - r + 9) / 2⌉ ≤ r`), and a black run of length `s ≥ 9` followed by a white run of length `r` and a black cell once `r ≥ 1 + 9 ⌈(k - 2) / 4⌉`, `k = P - s - r` (Theorem D: `r ≥ 1 + 9 ⌈(k + 8) / 2⌉`). So every repeating word `1^r u` with `1 ≤ \|u\| ≤ 4` and `u` starting white is excluded for `r ≥ 9` (`Rule30BlackRunBlocks`: `r ≥ 18` or `r ≥ 27` for its eight blocks), and with `\|u\| ≤ 8` for `r ≥ 18`. Contributed by Renaud Bosdure as Rule 30 Prize submission [`R30-20261027-317c64`](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261027-317c64). AI used: Gemini (Google) and Claude (Anthropic). | `no_long_black_run_quarter`, `no_black_white_run_quarter`, `no_black_run_ge9_len_le4`, `no_black_run_ge18_len_le8` |
| `Rule30ZeroBoundary` (`Q01_iff_integer`) | 2026-10-31 | `Q01` as an integer iteration. Read a finite start of the zero-boundary left half as the natural number `Y` whose bit `i` is the cell at depth `i + 1`: one step is `F Y = (Y >>> 1) ^^^ (Y \|\|\| (Y <<< 1))`, and `Q01` holds iff every natural number reaches an even number under some iterate of `G = F ∘ F`. `Q01` stays open. Contributed by Renaud Bosdure as Rule 30 Prize submission [`R30-20261027-317c64`](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261027-317c64). | `Rule30ZeroWallDiagonals` | 2026-11-10 | Left diagonals of the zero-boundary left half, measured from the moving edge of a nonzero finite row (`E L m j t = zrow L t (m + t - j)`), obey the left-diagonal recurrence whenever the entry guard `j + 2 ≤ m + t` holds, equality included (`E_entry`, `E_tailLeft`). For every array with eventually black coordinates 0 and 1 and such an eventual recurrence (`TailLeft`), each coordinate has eventual period `2 ^ j` (`tail_periodic`), `Q`-periodic profiles obey `M + 3 ≤ 2 ^ (2 Q)` (`tail_profile_bound`), and eventually white and eventually black coordinates both occur at arbitrarily large index (`tail_white_unbounded`, `tail_no_two_white`, `tail_black_after_white`, `tail_black_unbounded`, `E_black_unbounded`). Hence the free word of a finitely supported constrained row has bounded lag for bounded-slack repeats (`freeWord_bounded_lag`, `freeWord_bounded_lag_of_finSupp`), and, with the binary special-factor count (`factorCount_succ`, known) and an explicit two-run witness (`two_run`), its factor complexity exceeds `n + K` eventually for every `K` (`complexity_of_bounded_lag`, `freeWord_complexity`). Such rows exist only if `Q01` fails; the free-word statements are conditional. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-144e6e](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-144e6e). | `E_entry`, `E_tailLeft`, `tail_periodic`, `tail_profile_bound`, `tail_white_unbounded`, `tail_no_two_white`, `tail_black_after_white`, `tail_black_unbounded`, `E_black_unbounded`, `row_eq_Theta`, `rows_agree`, `freeWord_bounded_lag`, `freeWord_bounded_lag_of_finSupp`, `le_factorCount`, `factorCount_le`, `factorCount_succ`, `rightSpecialCount_pos`, `two_run`, `complexity_of_bounded_lag`, `freeWord_complexity` |
| `Rule30SettledBarrier` | 2026-10-28 | Settled white left diagonals obstruct long constant center runs. For every prescribed additive margin M, both 5L+M <= 3a for black runs and L+M <= a for white runs hold at all sufficiently large starting times. Contributed by @AIOvernight. AI used: OpenAI Codex and Claude (Anthropic); single-pair parity obstruction (`L + k ≤ a + 2` from a single settled pair `(k, k + 2)`) and generalized constant-diagonal barriers proved by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261103-462dc9](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261103-462dc9). | `black_run_margin_unbounded`, `white_run_margin_unbounded`, `no_pat_at_constant`, `no_nalt_at_constant`, `black_run_at_const_step`, `black_run_bound_at_black`, `preRow_pair_pin_even`, `white_run_pair_front_even`, `white_run_bound_one_pair_odd`, `white_run_bound_single_pair` |
| `Rule30FrontLaw` | 2026-10-28 | Exact transient-front reset law for left diagonals, explicit eventual periodicity from frontBound, and bounds on frontBound by sums of available periods. Contributed by @AIOvernight. AI used: OpenAI Codex and Claude (Anthropic); exact small-depth front bounds (`frontBound 2 = 2`, `frontBound 3 = 2`, `frontBound 4 ≤ 6`) and sharpened `frontBound k + 10 ≤ 2 ^ k` periodicity onset proved by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261103-050b68](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261103-050b68). | `D_front_law`, `D_periodic_from_frontBound`, `frontBound_le_periodSum`, `D_two_eq_false`, `frontBound_two`, `frontBound_three`, `frontBound_four`, `frontBound_five`, `periodSum_two_pow`, `frontBound_le_two_pow`, `frontBound_add_twenty_seven_le_two_pow`, `D_periodic_from_two_pow_sub_twenty_seven` |
| `Rule30SettledBarrierEffective` | 2026-10-27 | Explicit uniform bounds for every constant run of the single-seed center. A black run of length L starting at a >= 16 satisfies 5L + 3 log2(log2 a) <= 3a + 21; a white run starting at a >= 256 satisfies L + log2(log2 a) <= a + 2. Both logarithms are floor logarithms. Also gives parameterized thresholds, using settled left diagonals, a profile-injectivity bound and the transient front law. Supporting results have separate catalog rows. Contributed by @AIOvernight. AI used: OpenAI Codex and Claude (Anthropic); exact bounded `whiteCount` witnesses and sub-linear window existence of white, black, and both center colors proved by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261103-1c93e6](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261103-1c93e6), and sub-linear center transition existence, falling-edge extraction, and column-`(-1)` black-cell forcing ([R30-20261106-2b31c4](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261106-2b31c4)). | `black_run_effective`, `black_run_loglog`, `white_run_effective`, `white_run_loglog`, `whiteCount_pred_of_evWhite`, `exists_exact_whiteCount_settled`, `dec_all_center_true`, `dec_all_center_false`, `exists_white_in_window_effective`, `exists_white_in_window_loglog`, `exists_black_in_window_effective`, `exists_black_in_window_loglog`, `exists_both_colors_in_window_effective`, `exists_both_colors_in_window_loglog`, `exists_adj_ne_of_false_and_true`, `exists_falling_edge_of_true_before_false`, `exists_transition_in_window_effective`, `exists_transition_in_window_loglog`, `cell_neg1_of_center_true`, `exists_falling_edge_and_col_neg1_true_loglog` |
| `Rule30WhiteSpacing` | 2026-10-27 | Eventually white left diagonals are separated by at least five; a gap of five can only start at index two. Thus 6 whiteCount j <= j + 5, where whiteCount j counts the eventually white left diagonals of index at most j (Rule30LeftReset.whiteCount). For every actual black center run from a >= 16, 5L + 18 log2(log2 a) <= 3a + 51; for every white run from a >= 256, L + 6 log2(log2 a) <= a + 17. Same log-log order, larger coefficient and different intercepts; no density or nonperiodicity claim. Contributed by @AIOvernight as Rule 30 Prize submission `R30-20261027-e324b6`. AI used: OpenAI Codex and Claude (Anthropic); closed-form asymptotics of `D 2, …, D 7`, equivalence `(EvWhite (D j) ∧ EvWhite (D (j + 5))) ↔ j = 2`, exact `whiteCount` values through `j = 12`, and sharpness `6 * whiteCount 7 = 7 + 5` proved by G.P.J.W. | `Rule30FreeMiddle` | 2026-11-10 | Survival short of the whole row does not restrict additive ranks of the zero-boundary left half. For `u` tending to infinity, a finite row of width `W` is late (`Late`) if its double step survives and depth `1` is black at every even time up to `W - u W`. A rank is any integer function of (width, wall window, edge window) plus an integer density summed over the `m`-cell windows of the row (`rank`). If a rank is at least `-K W` on late rows and never increases along their double steps, the density is conserved on every closed walk (`free_middle`), hence a constant plus a shift coboundary by `Rule30LocalConservation` (`free_middle_coboundary`), and the rank is a function of (width, wall window, edge window) once both windows have at least `m - 1` cells (`rank_local`). So no rank of this form is nonnegative on late rows and strictly decreases along their double steps (`no_additive_ranking_late`); for functions of (width, wall window, edge window) alone this is `Rule30ZeroBoundaryBarriers.no_local_ranking` for the late population (`no_local_ranking_late`). The lower bound is necessary: popcount minus `W ^ 2` strictly decreases along every surviving double step of every finite row (`lower_bound_needed`). The proof plants a periodic word behind a stripe prefix, in the part of the row that survival through `W - u W` never reads, and uses the balance of the four-step image (`wsum_rho`, `cyclic_conserved_of_rho`). Bounded margins are not covered. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-a6ada9](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-a6ada9). | `no_local_ranking_late`, `wsum_rho`, `cyclic_conserved_of_rho`, `free_middle`, `free_middle_coboundary`, `rank_local`, `no_additive_ranking_late`, `lower_bound_needed` |

The right half whose cells `1..48` are its state `0` stays in it for ever by `PTrap.invariant` (`stays`), so it never enters `t001`: `¬ AbsorptionT t001` (`not_absorptionT_t001`). `not_eventually_001_of_absorption` stays true but its hypothesis is false, so the route for `001` needs a weaker entry statement. The theorem and the trap are Logan Hallee's, in the external `lhallee/rule30-open` (`Rule30Open.Columns.Traps001`, which also shows that entry into one of any three of four pairwise apart traps fails); the half named there is the state `25` of this table (`rule30open_half`). | `Rule30HybridWall` | 2026-11-10 | The immortal rows of a linear relative of rule 30, exactly. The alternating 90/150 hybrid `H` with a white wall applies rule 150 at odd depths and rule 90 at even depths; a row is immortal when its depth-1 cell is black at every even time. A finite row is immortal iff `x 1 = 1`, `x (4 j + 3) = x (4 j + 5)` and `x (4 j + 2) = x (4 j + 1) + x (4 j + 3)` for every `j` (`immortal_iff_bitFam_anyWall`, `immortal_iff_famRow`); in powers of `A = S + S⁻¹` these are the rows `(A + A²) p(A⁴) + r(A⁴)` with `p 0 = 1`, `r 0 = 0` (`immortal_real_iff`). For `W ≥ 2` the immortal rows of width at most `W` are in bijection with the Boolean words supported below `W / 2 - 1` (`immortal_iff_param`), and their count `2 ^ (W / 2 - 1)` is kernel-checked for `W ≤ 12`; the width-6 ones are `3, 11, 53, 61` (`immortal_width_six`). The column of every nonzero finite row is not eventually periodic (`col_not_eventually_periodic`). The proof is the decimation semiconjugacy `D (P (P x)) = P (D x)` of the pair model `P (a, b) = (a + b, T a)` (`D_P_P`, `psi_even_time`). The hybrid is not rule 30. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-41fdb3](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-41fdb3). | `D_P_P`, `psi_even_time`, `even_time_constant`, `psi_mono`, `iter_H_real`, `col_real`, `colE_even`, `col_inj`, `colE_inj`, `real_surj`, `real_inj`, `immortal_real_iff`, `real_afam`, `immortal_iff_famRow`, `immortal_iff_bitFam_anyWall`, `immortal_iff_param`, `immortal_width_six`, `not_immortal_width_one`, `col_not_eventually_periodic`, `immortal_odd_word_not_eventually_periodic` |

| `Rule30LinearCharts` | 2026-11-11 | Every linear chart of the constrained zero-boundary starts is the free-bit chart. On `KK L := L 0 = false ∧ InX L`, `Theta` and the free-bit read-off `fb` are inverse bijections with all sequences that turn two steps into the one-sided shift (`kk_Theta`, `fb_Theta`, `Theta_fb`, `kk_two_step`, `fb_shift`, `uld_Theta`, `uld_fb`). If `A` is closed under `a + b + c` (pointwise xor), `Lm` is affine on `A`, and `Φ`, `Φ'` are mutually inverse between `KK` and `A`, uniformly finitely dependent, with `Φ (zrow L 2) = Lm (Φ L)`, then `Φ ∘ Theta` is affine (`linear_chart_is_free_bit_chart`, `free_bit_chart_affine`, `free_bit_chart_iso`; `free_bit_chart_is_chart` checks the free-bit chart itself). The proof is the group-law lemma `groupLaw_eq_xor`: a commutative operation on one-sided sequences with the white sequence as neutral element, `(x + y) + y = x`, commuting with the shift and with bit 0 fixed by a finite window, is pointwise xor (`fibre_form`, `involution_Q`, `flip_invariant`, `descent_step`, `window_zero`, `q_white`). Continuity is represented as uniform finite dependence; its equivalence with continuity on compact domains is not formalized. The automorphism theorem of the one-sided full 2-shift is due to Hedlund (1969); for topological group laws the rigidity also follows from Wibmer's Corollary 4.4 (arXiv:2008.00755) with Hedlund's theorem, and the module gives a direct proof. Nothing here decides `Q01` or period 2. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261111-0121a4](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261111-0121a4). | `groupLaw_eq_xor`, `fibre_form`, `involution_Q`, `flip_invariant`, `descent_step`, `window_zero`, `q_white`, `kk_Theta`, `fb_Theta`, `Theta_fb`, `kk_two_step`, `fb_shift`, `uld_Theta`, `uld_fb`, `linear_chart_is_free_bit_chart`, `free_bit_chart_affine`, `free_bit_chart_iso`, `free_bit_chart_is_chart` |

| `Rule30PeriodTwoSideways` | 2026-10-17 | The coupled question `CQ01` in two more forms. It holds iff no configuration with finitely many black cells has center column `1, 0, 1, 0, …` from time 0. Sideways: for every finite right half `R`, one explicit left row `Psi R` (the constrained start whose free bits complement column 1 of the driven right half) has infinitely many black cells; this holds for `R` whenever it holds for the right half two steps later, or whenever column 1 of the driven right half is eventually periodic at the white steps. Exact death: a left row keeps the center alternating exactly until the first depth where it differs from `Psi R`. States the `R = ∅` milestone `CQ01Empty` (**open**). Barrier: from finite configurations the center alternates arbitrarily long, so no ranking through a finite abstraction of configurations decreases at every double step over which the center flips twice. | `CQ01_iff_no_finite_alternating`, `Q01_no_leftfinite_alternating`, `CQ01_iff_Psi`, `not_finSupp_Psi_of_R2`, `exact_death_center`, `truncation_death`, `CQ01Empty_iff`, `no_finite_abstract_ranking_coupled` |
| `Rule30FiniteConfigs` | 2026-10-17 | The single-seed theorems in their uniform form. For every configuration with a leftmost black cell (nonzero and left-finite): Jen's theorem (at most one column is eventually periodic) and period 1 (no column is eventually constant); the proofs use the seed only through its left edge. For nonzero finite configurations: `CQ01` holds iff none has an eventually 2-periodic center column, whatever the transient, so `CQ01` is exactly FinConf at period 2 for one column. `Q01` gives the same for every configuration with a leftmost black cell. | `jen`, `no_column_eventually_constant`, `jen_finite`, `no_column_eventually_constant_finite`, `CQ01_iff_no_finite_period_two`, `no_leftFinite_period_two_of_Q01` |
| `Rule30RadiusTwoClock` | 2026-11-10 | Left permutivity at radius two does not exclude an exactly periodic column from a finite seed, even with two states. For Booleans `p`, `q` the rule `N p q` sets `new x i = x (i - 2) xor g (x (i - 1)) (x i) (x (i + 1)) (x (i + 2))`, where `g` is true unless its last three arguments are equal, except `g 1 1 0 1 = p` and `g 1 0 1 1 = q`; each rule is white-quiescent, nonlinear and not right-permutive (`rule_facts`). From exactly the black cells `0` and `2`, columns `-2` and `-1` are `t mod 2` at every time, for all four rules (`clock`); from `2` and `4`, column `0` alternates (`column0_alternates`). The proof is finite: the row is a doubled block field under rule 150 with two frontier sources (`row_formula`, `z_frontier`), and the field minus its reflection about block `-1` is an even band growing by two cells every two steps (`d_band`), so block `-1` flips at every step (`z_clock`). The rules are not rule 30. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-5f4dd3](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-5f4dd3). | `rule_facts`, `z_frontier`, `row_formula`, `d_band`, `z_clock`, `clock`, `column0_alternates` |
| `Rule30FiniteMaskedJen` | 2026-10-17 | The masked and window forms of Jen's theorem (`Rule30MaskedJen`) for every configuration with a leftmost black cell: if column `i` is eventually periodic, column `i + 1` is not periodic with the same period on the white times of column `i`, and `column i ∨ column (i + 1)` is not eventually periodic; with no periodicity assumed, two times never carry the same (column, or-column) window reaching the left edge; a periodic column has, in every window `[n, n + m)` with `n` past the transient and `m = n + i - lo`, a white time where the next column breaks the period. | `masked_jen`, `orCol_not_eventuallyPeriodic`, `window_jen_col`, `periodic_window` |
| `Rule30FiniteBlackRuns` | 2026-10-17 | The black-run exclusions of `Rule30BlackRuns`, for every configuration with a leftmost black cell and every column `i`. The nine-black shield holds in the evolution of every configuration; the exclusions then use the seed only through Jen's theorem and its masked form. If from some time on every black run of column `i` that is followed by white has length at least `9`, column `i` is not eventually periodic. If column `i` has period `P`, no period contains a black run of length `r` with `9 ⌈(P - r + 9) / 2⌉ ≤ r` (Theorem B), or a black run of length `≥ 9` followed by a long white run (Theorem D). The same for every nonzero finite configuration. | `nine_black`, `black_shield`, `not_eventuallyPeriodic_of_long_one_runs`, `no_long_black_run`, `no_black_white_run`, `not_eventuallyPeriodic_of_long_one_runs_finite` |

| `Rule30FiniteDiagonals` | 2026-10-17 | The periodic diagonals of `Rule30Diagonals`, for every configuration with an edge. If `x` is white right of `hi`, the diagonal `t ↦ evol x t (hi + t - j)` is periodic from time 0 with period dividing `2^j`. If `x` is white left of `lo`, the diagonal `t ↦ evol x t (lo - t + j)` has period `2^j` from the explicit onset `2^j-1`; the original eventual theorem remains. Every column reads its array of diagonals along a line of slope one, as the center column of the seed does. So the periodic right diagonals behind the 2-adic odometer are not special to the seed. The bitwise form, for every starting value of `Φ(x) = x ⊕ (2x ∨ 4x)`, is `Rule30Equivalents.TFunction`. | `Rule30LightCone` | 2026-10-17 | The locality behind space-efficient evaluation. A cell at time `t` depends only on the initial cells within distance `t`. With space blocks and time steps of width `b`, each block is a fixed function of the three blocks around it one time step earlier, so the center cell at time `m b` is the root of a fan-in-3 tree of `b`-bit blocks with height `m`. This is the shape that Williams's square-root-space simulation passes to Cook–Mertz tree evaluation, about `√n · log n` bits for the center cell at time `n`; neither algorithm is formalized; left/right light-cone boundary sensitivity, upper-triangular left-permutivity, and left-parent block-tree injectivity proved by G.P.J.W. | `Rule30FiveColumnBound` | 2026-11-10 | Every five adjacent physical Rule 30 columns contain at most `3 * T + 2` black cells in any interval of `T` ticks, for every configuration and starting time (`evol_five_column_bound`), including the official singleton (`cell_five_column_bound`). | `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeCascade` | 2026-10-30 | Universal four-stage backward wave cascade (`1^∞ → (100)^∞ → (011)^∞ → (000101)^∞`): every Type I primitive root enters the period-3 `(100)^∞` NOR wave at backward step 3 (`bstep1_nor_period_three`, `typeI_step3_nor_wave`, `typeI_eight_bit_three_phases`), maps to its bitwise complement `(011)^∞` at step 4 (`wave_100_to_011_step1`, `wave_100_to_011_step0`), and doubles its spatial period to 6 (`(000101)^∞`) under `bstep0` (`wave_011_to_period6_step0`). | `Rule30PlantedSurvivor` | 2026-11-10 | A different map with an immortal finite row can match arbitrarily large finite portions of rule 30's survival table. For `L ≥ 1`, `psi L` flips the cell at every odd depth `k ≥ 2 L + 1` at which the row agrees with the stripe `…10101` below `k`; it is a tree automorphism of the rows with inverse `psiInv L` (`psi_psiInv`, `psiInv_psi`), sends the stripe to the finite row `trunc (2 L - 1)` (`psi_stripe`), and its inverse does not increase the width of any finite row except that one (`psiInv_fin`). The map `T L`, the zero-boundary step read through `psi L` (`Tn_eq`), maps finite rows to finite rows (`T_finite`), fixes the empty row (`T_zero`) and fixes `trunc (2 L - 1)` (`T_fixed`), whose depth `1` is black at every time (`fixed_survives`). For every width bound `W` and time `t`, the rows of width at most `W` that pass every even check of depth `1` through `t` number the same under `T L` as under rule 30, plus one exactly when `2 L - 1 ≤ W` and `2 ⌈W / 2⌉ ≤ t` (`survival_table`). `T L` is not a cellular automaton and not rule 30. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-7a4a0d](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-7a4a0d). | `psi_psiInv`, `psiInv_psi`, `psi_stripe`, `psiInv_fin`, `Tn_eq`, `T_finite`, `T_zero`, `T_fixed`, `fixed_survives`, `survival_table` |
| `Rule30WhiteEnd` | 2026-10-23 | The white end. For every `q ≥ 10`, no column of rule 30 is eventually the repeating word `1 0^q`, whatever the transient: for the single seed, `EventuallyWord (true :: List.replicate q false)` is false, and the same holds for every column of every configuration with a leftmost black cell, every finite nonzero one included. `Rule30StripCertificateTable` excludes only `q = 10, 11, 12`. A one-sided strip relaxation (eight cells right of the column, an arbitrary cell beyond) determines column `c + 1` after three periods; kernel checks cover `q = 10 … 25`, and the relaxation's white step satisfies `W^26 = W^22` on the sets that occur, which covers every `q ≥ 26`. Two adjacent columns periodic for ever then contradict Jen's theorem with a clock. The checks are in `Rule30Certificates/WhiteEndChecks`, where `control_q9` shows that the check fails at `q = 9`. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude and GPT as Rule 30 Prize submission `R30-20261022-c27323`, and ported to core Lean. | `not_eventuallyWord_white_end`, `white_end_seed`, `white_end`, `white_end_finite` |
| `Rule30WhiteEndNine` | 2026-10-26 | The white end from `q = 9`, by one pumped mode certificate. For every `q ≥ 9`, the single-seed center column is not eventually the repeating word `1 0^q`, whatever the transient; `Rule30WhiteEnd` is not used. A *mode certificate* (`not_eventually_word_mode`, sound for every word, width, warm-up and label phase) lets column `1` carry one free bit per period at one white phase, the *label*, and checks two transition rules on the labels (after `1` comes `0`; after `0 0` comes `0`); the labels are then eventually 2-periodic and masked Jen gives the contradiction. For `1 0^q` the certificate is one formula in `q`: twelve cells, five warm-up periods, label phase `2`, the reachable set and two fixed label masks. Sets closed under white steps, checked once, cover every period of every `1 0^q` with `q ≥ 10` (`modeCert_pumped`, `pumpCheck_12`); at `q = 9`, where the label `1` occurs, the kernel evaluates the same formula (`modeCert_nine`), and `pumpCheck_12_nine` shows that the same pumped check fails from nine white steps. Set images are computed bit-parallel (`fimage`, proved sound), so the module builds in a few seconds. Contributed by AIOvernight with Claude (Anthropic) as Rule 30 Prize submission `R30-20261026-c389d5`. | `not_eventually_white_end_ge9`, `modeCert_white_end`, `modeCert_pumped`, `not_eventually_word_mode` |

| `Rule30TraceMerge` | 2026-10-24 | Exact obstruction to stronger left reconstruction after dropping initial right finiteness: two rows with edge -3 and different initial cells at -1 have identical entire center traces, merge after one step, and become finite after two. Both initial rows are infinite; their edges agree. Also proves that edge recovery for eventually finite rows is equivalent to the open finite-row hypothesis. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `no_left_recovery_from_eventual_finiteness`, `eventual_finite_edge_iff` |
| `Rule30TraceAmbiguity` | 2026-10-24 | For every horizon H, two finite initial rows with edge -3 and different initial cells at -1 agree on the first H center observations. Thus no horizon depending only on left depth recovers the initial left half when right support is unbounded. In contrast, four observations recover the left neighbor at depth two, for arbitrary right halves. This does not refute finite-row recovery from an entire infinite trace or edge recovery. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `arbitrarily_long_finite_left_ambiguity`, `no_uniform_finite_left_recovery`, `recover_left_neighbor_at_depth_two`, `left_neighbor_rigid_at_depth_two` |
| `Rule30SpacingRenormalization` | 2026-10-24 | Exact eight-column reconstruction identity under gate spacing at least three, with finite-support transfer. The spacing survives its transform exactly when the original gate is white at every index at least four; a period-five gate disproves unconditional iteration at depth seventeen. Does not exclude period two. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex, following the one-step identity in [morpholib Theorem 14](https://github.com/fractastical/morpholib/blob/main/docs/RULE30_PRIZE_SUBMISSION.md); unconditional closed forms at depths 2, 3, and 4 (`pair_two`, `pair_three`, `pair_four`), zero-collision disjunction identity `transform G r = (G (r + 2) \|\| G (r + 4))` (`transform_eq_or`), closed-form hierarchy for depths 4 through 12 under `Spaced G` (`pair_four_spaced` .. `pair_twelve_spaced`), exact 3-gap characterization at depth 12 (`recon_twelve_even_eq_true_iff_gap_three`), and the direct 8-depth renormalization bridge and finite-support transfer for `Rule30ZeroBoundary.Theta` (`boundary_succ_eq_alt`, `recon_boundary_one_eq_Theta`, `Theta_spaced_translate`, `Theta_support_transfer`, `Theta_finite_transfer`), by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-5795f4](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-5795f4). | `reconstruction_translate`, `finite_transfer`, `transform_spaced_iff`, `second_translation_fails`, `pair_two`, `pair_three`, `pair_four`, `transform_eq_or`, `pair_four_spaced`, `pair_eight_spaced`, `pair_eleven_spaced`, `pair_twelve_spaced`, `recon_twelve_even_eq_true_iff_gap_three`, `boundary_succ_eq_alt`, `recon_boundary_one_eq_Theta`, `Theta_spaced_translate`, `Theta_support_transfer`, `Theta_finite_transfer` |
| `Rule30PackedReconstruction` | 2026-10-24 | A finite packed temporal-column computation agrees with the scalar sideways reconstruction inside its represented light cone. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `packed_sound` |
| `Rule30SpacingGapCounterexample` | 2026-10-24 | A period-31 gate has successive black spacings only three or five, yet its reconstructed initial row is white at all depths 528 through 544. Rules out extrapolating a sixteen-cell white-gap bound to all depths; makes no finite-right realizability claim. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `gate31_gaps`, `gate31_spaced`, `seventeen_white`, `no_uniform_gap_bound_sixteen` |
| `Rule30DrivenPrefix` | 2026-10-24 | Packed spatial rows of the alternating-driven right half, with exact bit soundness, prefix dependence and finite gate-window observations. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex; stepwise period-5 forcing on 3-gap-free `Window` stretches (`spaced_of_window`, `window_step_five_of_no_gap_three`), quantitative `free_bits_clock` upper bounds on 3-gap droughts and cofinal 3-gap existence (`no_gap_three_clock_bound`, `cofinal_gap_three_of_finSupp_InX`), 8-step minimum spacing between 3-gaps under no-`33` (`gap_three_spacing_eight_of_no_33`), and multi-step shift conjugacy and direct 8-depth renormalization of `Psi R` (`Theta_shift_two_mul`, `Psi_shift_two_mul`, `Psi_spaced_translate`, `finSupp_Theta_transform_of_finSupp_Psi`), by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-901d7a](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-901d7a). | `packed_sound`, `double_prefix`, `iterate_mod_bit`, `spaced_of_window`, `window_step_five_of_no_gap_three`, `no_gap_three_clock_bound`, `cofinal_gap_three_of_finSupp_InX`, `gap_three_spacing_eight_of_no_33`, `Theta_shift_two_mul`, `Psi_shift_two_mul`, `Psi_spaced_translate`, `finSupp_Theta_transform_of_finSupp_Psi` |
| `Rule30ExactReturn` | 2026-10-24 | The full eight-column return holds exactly under spacing with only the initial distance-two pair exempted. For a spaced gate, this condition survives transformation exactly when the gate is white from index five onward; arbitrarily late black observations block a second return. Includes the sharp boundary example. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `translation_iff_nearSpaced`, `transformed_exactReturn_iff`, `infinite_gate_fails_arbitrarily_late`, `lastAllowed_transformed_return` |
| `Rule30WallDeadlines` | 2026-11-10 | `Q01` answered exactly for two linear relatives of rule 30. Beside a white wall every rule `left xor h (centre, right)` has the frame `zr h`, with `zr (or) = zrow` (`zr_or_eq_zrow`). For rule 90, if depth `1` is black at every even tick up to `T`, every cell with depth plus tick odd is Fibonacci modulo `2` of its depth (`odd90`, `fib2_mod`), so a start of width at most `W` turns depth `1` white by tick `kstar W - 1`, the least odd number above `W` not divisible by `3` less one (`kstar_spec`, `deadline90`), and every start whose odd depths follow Fibonacci attains it (`sharp90`). For rule 150 two steps act on the odd depths by their own rule (`two_step150`), every odd depth is forced black (`odd150`), the deadline is `2 * ((W + 1) / 2)` (`deadline150`), and every start with all odd depths black attains it (`sharp150`). These rules are not rule 30. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-c20b0a](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-c20b0a). | `zr_or_eq_zrow`, `fib2_mod`, `odd90`, `kstar_spec`, `deadline90`, `sharp90`, `two_step150`, `odd150`, `deadline150`, `sharp150` |
| `Rule30ControlledResponse` | 2026-10-24 | Exact equivalence between responses to every left-boundary input and head/OR observations of the triangular diagonal map. Terminal rewrites `011 -> 01` and `101 -> 11` preserve these responses behind any common prefix and preserve the full Rule 30 center trace with any common initial left half. Includes explicit limits of short-window and fixed-driver tests. Normal-form completeness is not claimed. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `sameResponse_iff_diagonal`, `tail_011_drv`, `tail_101_drv`, `feedback_left_equal`, `tail_011_center`, `tail_101_center` |
| `Rule30ResponseAncestry` | 2026-11-06 | Finite nonzero rows with the same entire center trace have exactly the same finite ancestry depths if their center-including right tails have the same universal controlled response, or enter the same response class after any finite time. | `Rule30CenterRuns` | 2026-10-24 | Runs of the center column are at most linear in time. A block of `L` consecutive cells of one color starting at time `a ≥ 1` (not necessarily maximal) has `L ≤ a` if black, attained at `a = 3`, and `L ≤ a - 1` if white, attained only at `a = 2`; for `a ≥ 3` a white block has `L ≤ a - 3`, not attained up to time 3000. So every window `[a, 2a]` holds a white cell and every window `[a, 2a)` a black cell (`[a, 2a - 2)` for `a ≥ 3`), and the counts grow at least logarithmically: `m + 1` black cells before `2^m`, so `Nat.log2 n ≤ blackCount n`, and `m` white cells before `4^m`. The black bound holds for every column of every configuration with a leftmost black cell, measured from the time the left edge passed the column. Black runs: left-permutivity forces the alternating row `… 0 1 0 1` under the run, which cannot cover the two adjacent leftmost black cells. White runs: the first black cell right of the center (`Rule30BlackRuns.front_moves`) fixes column `1`, which forces the row at the start of the run in closed form; it contradicts the left-edge diagonals `D 1`, …, `D 4` of `Rule30Diagonals`. Contributed by Luis Perez Morales, Claude (Fable 5.1), as Rule 30 Prize submission `R30-20261024-03b551` (superseding `R30-20261024-801153`, whose bounds were `L ≤ 3a - 2` for white runs and a black cell in every `[a, 4a)`), and ported to core Lean. Sharper bounds are in `Rule30BlackRunErosion` (`L + 2⌊(a - 1)/5⌋ ≤ a` for black blocks) and `Rule30WhiteSpacing` (a margin growing like `log₂ log₂ a` for both colors). | `black_run_le`, `white_run_le'`, `white_run_le_three`, `exists_white`, `exists_black'`, `exists_black_three`, `blackCount_pow_two`, `log_two_succ_le_blackCount`, `log_two_le_blackCount`, `whiteCount_pow_four`, `log_le_whiteCount`, `black_run_le_evol` |

| `Rule30EtherThreeErosion` | 2026-10-24 | The period-3 ether erodes, so it has no wall of any width. Erosion: let a rule-30 evolution of the columns `p ≥ 1` have column `0` driven by the 12 × 3 ether. If at some time the columns `1..k` carry the ether, the columns beyond `k + w` carry the ether, and some column does not, then within `3 w + 6` steps one of the columns `1..k` leaves the ether. No wall: for every attachment column `a` and every width `b ≥ 1`, `WallOK a b W` forces `W` empty. This extends `no_wall_le_12` of `Rule30EtherThreeNoWall` to every width, so `absorption3_excludes` is vacuous and the ether-and-wall route to least period 3 is closed. `Q001`, `C3s001` and `C3w(011)` do not involve walls and stay open; `AbsorptionT t001` is false (`Rule30EtherThreeNotAbsorption`). The leftmost defect (the front) has 24 states (class of its column mod 12, and whether the next column is a defect). A kernel check of 576 local patterns (`cert_all`, `decide +kernel`) shows one bad state, which alone can jump right by 3 or more and re-enters itself only by jumping at least 8. On the other 23 states a potential `ψ ≤ 10` makes `5 D + 2 t + ψ` non-increasing (`front_step`, `wake_good`). The rightmost defect moves right by exactly one per step (`redge_step`, left-permutivity), which bounds the run of bad steps (`run_bad`). A wall state plus one flipped cell then contradicts `trap_invariant3`. | `Rule30White17Runs` | 2026-10-27 | Every white run at least 17, with black runs of every length. For every nonempty list of blocks `(r, q)` with `r ≥ 1` and `q ≥ 17`, chosen independently, the single-seed center column is not eventually the repeating word `1^(r₁) 0^(q₁) … 1^(rₖ) 0^(qₖ)`, whatever the transient. The number of blocks and every run length are unbounded. So every eventual period of the center that is not constant has a cyclic white run of length at most 16. `Rule30BlackRuns` needs black runs of length `≥ 9`; here short black runs are mixed freely, with no dominating white run. Two blocks capture every fourteen-cell strip into a 683-strip envelope (`16384 → 845 → 683`), split into nine rank regions; no block raises the rank, so the rank is eventually constant along successive periods (`Rule30RankedWords.no_word_ranked`, a composition rule for any width). A rank-keeping block has a prescribed column-1 response along its white run (rank 0: delay `2, 0, 4, 0` for `r = 1 … 4`, `1` for `r ≥ 5`; ranks 4 and 8 keep only `r = 3`, delays `3` and `10`), and masked Jen concludes. All family data, finite checks and assembly are in `Rule30Certificates/White17`; transitions use the proved-sound bit-parallel `Rule30FastImage.fcovers`, while rank predicates and observations retain the sparse checker. The pure-black-tail bound 17 is exposed in `Rule30PaddedWords`. Author: @AIOvernight. AI used: OpenAI Codex, Claude. | `not_eventually_long_white_runs` |
| `Rule30GrammarRuns` | 2026-10-27 | Four-category block grammars. The number of blocks, the white lengths and the long black lengths are unbounded. `Rule30BlackRuns` (`not_eventuallyPeriodic_of_long_one_runs`) is the case where every block has `r ≥ 9`; here such blocks mix freely with blocks of one, two or four black cells, and with white runs of 7 to 16 cells. Four capture stages take every fourteen-cell strip into an envelope of 1791 strips (`16384 → 2288 → 1915 → 1797 → 1791`), split into seven rank regions of sizes `1386, 339, 44, 13, 2, 5, 2`. No block raises the rank, and a rank-keeping block either starts at rank 0, where column `1` along its white run is white for `d = 2, 0, 0, 1` steps (`r = 1, 2, 4, ≥ 9`) and then black, or is a block `1^4 0^q` at rank 6 (`d = 1`). `Rule30RankedWords.no_word_ranked` (a composition rule for any width) and masked Jen conclude. The 557 finite checks and masks reside in one `Rule30Certificates/Grammar` module; transition inclusions use the arbitrary-width, proved-sound `Rule30FastImage` checker and kernel evaluation. | `Rule30FirstChecks` | 2026-11-06 | The first composite check past the free range, exactly. Writing `check n l = Theta (word l) (2 n + 1)` and `ones n` for the number of words of length `n` on which it is black: the rows of width at most `2 n` black at column `-1` at every even tick through `2 n` number `2 ^ n - ones n` (`first_check_count`), and the check differs from the check of the shifted word on exactly `2 ^ m + ones m` of the words of length `m + 2` (`pair_count`), from a two-tick identity and `tri_odd`, `tri_even`, `Theta_dep`. With the overlap bounds on two sets this gives `adjacent` and `distance_two`, so at most one of any three consecutive checks from `2` on is near constant (`two_of_three`), and for the others the share of rows that pass lies in `[1/40, 39/40]` (`first_check_balanced`). First check only: from the second check the fresh bits are pinned, so the bounds do not multiply. It proves no bound on later checks and not `Q01`. | `Rule30FirstCheckDegree` | 2026-11-10 | Two algebraic normal form coefficient laws and the exact degree of `Rule30FirstChecks.check` for every positive `n`. With `coeff` the subcube exclusive-or and `anf_expansion` its Möbius inversion, the coefficient of `f 0 ⋯ f (n - 1)` is `n mod 2` for `n ≥ 1` (`top_coefficient`) and the coefficient of the monomial without `f 1` is `n + 1 mod 2` for `n ≥ 2` (`omit_coefficient`), so the check has degree `n` for odd `n` and `n - 1` for even `n` (`check_degree`). Hence `ones n ≡ n` and, with `first_check_count`, `firstCheck n ≡ n` modulo `2` for `n ≥ 1` (`ones_parity`, `first_check_parity`); the Walsh sum `2 ^ n - 2 * ones n` is `2` modulo `4` exactly at odd `n ≥ 2` and `0` at `n = 1` (`walsh_mod_four`, `walsh_one`). In the other clock phase, free bits at the even steps and column `-1` black at the odd ones, the check at depth `2 n` has odd weight for every `n ≥ 1` (`other_phase_odd`). The proof differentiates along the anti-diagonals of the reconstruction (`diag`): summing out `f 0` leaves two cells in the kit phase (`top_succ`) and one in the other. | `Rule30SurvivalBridge` | 2026-11-01 | A repeat budget is mortality of the auxiliary frontier. A frontier is a finite word over `{0,1,2,3}` read from two adjacent anti-diagonals of a Rule 30 diagram, updated by a partial scan `Z` that keeps every earlier guard. A repeat budget at length `r` is mortality at length `r` in both directions (`budgetAt_iff_mortalAt`, `budget_iff_mortality`), and a repeat bound at every capacity gives mortality for every capacity function at once (`mortality_of_capacity_budget`). The run bound `runBound` and the survival inequality `survival` are proved, not assumed. Control `survival_counting_fails_without_runBound`: the counting inequality is false for tapes in general. Mortality implies `Rule30PeriodTwo.Q01` (`Rule30AlternatingTraceBridge.Q01_of_mortality`); the converse is not known. It proves no budget, capacity bound or mortality. Contributed by David Condrey; left-prefix autonomy, 1-step memory erasure, and front/terminal causal cone collision by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261102-715db1](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261102-715db1), and the left-diagonal conjugacy by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as [R30-20261102-121ada](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261102-121ada); ANF decomposition, marginal causal influence census, and spacetime retrodiction by G.P.J.W. | `Rule30AlternatingTraceBridge` | 2026-11-01 | Mortality of the auxiliary frontier excludes a nonconstant period-two center. A nonzero finite row whose center trace satisfies `c(t + 2) = c(t)` and `c(1) ≠ c(0)` from time zero yields a legal finite frontier that passes every chronological guard (`immortalFromAlternating`), so mortality excludes it (`periodTwoExcluded_of_mortality'`). For the official center column, any such exclusion for finite rows (`center_not_eventually_period_two_of_excluded`), and so mortality (`center_not_eventually_period_two_of_mortality`), implies that no transient `T` gives `c(t + 2) = c(t)` for all `t ≥ T`. Mortality implies `Rule30PeriodTwo.Q01` (`Q01_of_mortality`), by feeding a counterexample to `Q01` (a zero-driven left half whose column `-1` is black at every even step, with the center alternating) to the same reconstruction, which uses only the columns left of the center (`immortalFromHistory`); the converse is not known, since mortality ranges over every legal frontier. Control `alternatingCenter_without_finiteSupport`: the 7-periodic row with ones at `j ≡ 6 (mod 7)` has center `0, 1, 0, 1, …`, so finiteness cannot be dropped. Mortality is not proved here; Problem 1 stays open. | `Rule30FrontierChart` | 2026-11-06 | `Q01` implies mortality, so `Q01`, mortality and a repeat budget at every length are one statement. An immortal frontier is read as a space-time chart: the high bit of depth `e` of the `n`-th frontier at time `2 n - e`, the low bit one tick earlier. The scan recurrences of `Z` are the Rule 30 recurrence at every depth of at least two (`Z_rec`, `chart_interior`), and the two terminal symbols give it at depth one with a white wall from time one on (`chart_wall`). So the row at time two is a finitely supported start of the zero-boundary left half with column `-1` black at every even step (`zrow_chart`, `finSupp_start`, `inX_start`), which `Rule30ZeroBoundary.Q01` forbids. It proves no budget, capacity bound or mortality. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261106-45f6e9](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261106-45f6e9). | `mortality_of_Q01`, `mortality_iff_Q01`, `budget_iff_Q01`, `Z_rec`, `chart_interior`, `chart_wall`, `zrow_chart`, `inX_start`, `finSupp_start` |
| `Rule30WedgeBridge` | 2026-11-01 | The binary wedge hypothesis and the rotated wedge on streams over `Fin 4`: `phi` is the backward step on pairs of adjacent cells, `cone`/`inverse` the inverse cone of a boundary stream, `wedge n e = peel^n (inverse e)`. `BWH` (open): for `n ≥ 7` no stream with a binary prefix of length `2n + 2` has a constant wedge in `{2, 3}` of length `n + 2`. `RW` (open) is the rotated form with a hard-core junction. `BWH` and the six small lengths `SmallRW` imply `RW` (`bwh_implies_rw`), and `RW` truncates to `BWH` (`rw_truncates_to_bwh`); `maximal_drop_inheritance` is the finite-horizon step. Controls show the small cases and the hard-core guard cannot be dropped. Neither hypothesis is proved. Contributed by David Condrey. Polynomial-time cone/wedge row-stepping (`collectHead_tabulate_cone`, `fastWedgeList_eq`) and unconditional proof of `SmallRW` (`smallRW`) discharging `SmallRW` from `BWH → RW` (`bwh_implies_rw_unconditional`) contributed by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini ([R30-20261102-f48894](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261102-f48894)). | `bwh_implies_rw`, `rw_truncates_to_bwh`, `maximal_drop_inheritance`, `small_control_table`, `hard_core_guard_control`, `small_bwh_control`, `collectHead_tabulate_cone`, `fastWedgeList_eq`, `no_hc_wedge_of_cert`, `smallRW`, `bwh_implies_rw_unconditional` |
| `Rule30SeparatorBridge` | 2026-11-01 | `RW` implies two separators for hard-core streams (binary symbols, no two adjacent `1`), with no finite census: no hard-core `e` has `inverse e` eventually constant in `{2, 3}` (`rw_implies_constant_tail_separator`), and none has `inverse e = liftWord u (const c)` for a word `u` over `{0, 1, 2}` (`rw_implies_orbit_separator`, `constant_separator_implies_full_orbit_separator`). Control `no_pull_without_hard_core_control`: the hard-core condition is needed. `RW` is not proved. | `Rule30UnrestrictedClosing` | 2026-11-10 | Unrestricted tail closing fails on the constrained class `InX` of the zero-boundary left half. Three eventually periodic starts read from depth 1: `rowA` repeats `1011111`, `rowB` is `11` followed by repeats of `01001011001000`, and `rowC` repeats `1100001`. Two ticks map `A` and `B` to `C` and `C` to `A` at every depth (`two_A`, `two_B`, `two_C`, `two_cycle`), so all three are in `InX` (`inX_A`, `inX_B`, `inX_C`); their free words are `1010…`, `001010…` and `0101…` (`free_A`, `free_B`, `free_C`), and `Theta_unique` identifies each row with `Theta` of its free word (`theta_A`, `theta_B`, `theta_C`). `A` and `B` differ at every depth `14 q + 3` (`differ_AB`) and one tick later at every depth `14 q + 4` (`differ_one_tick`). Hence two rows of `InX` with the same row after one double step need not agree on any tail (`not_unrestricted_closing`). This does not contradict the guarded closing statements of open pull request 52, which assume legal gate streams: the common future here leaves their legal windows at once. Nothing here decides `Q01` or period 2. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-497a64](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-497a64); exact free-tail characterization of `2 * m`-tick collisions on `InX` (`inX_and_free_shift`, `zrow_two_mul_eq_iff_free_tail_eq`), single-bit discrepancy `fA s = fB s ↔ s ≠ 0` (`fA_eq_fB_iff`), full coalescence `zrow rowA t k = zrow rowB t k` for `t ≥ 2` (`zrow_rowA_eq_zrow_rowB_of_ge_two`), `4`-tick periodicity of `rowA` and `rowC` (`four_tick_cycle_AC`), and proof that `gateOf rowA`, `gateOf rowB`, and `gateOf rowC` violate minimum-gap-`3` spacing `SpacedFrom S₀` on every tail (`gateOf`, `SpacedFrom`, `not_spacedFrom_gateOf_rowA_rowB_rowC`), by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261111-63a2fc](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261111-63a2fc). | `two_A`, `two_B`, `two_C`, `two_cycle`, `inX_A`, `inX_B`, `inX_C`, `free_A`, `free_B`, `free_C`, `theta_A`, `theta_B`, `theta_C`, `differ_AB`, `differ_one_tick`, `not_unrestricted_closing`, `zrow_rowA_eq_zrow_rowB_of_ge_two`, `four_tick_cycle_AC`, `fA_eq_fB_iff`, `inX_and_free_shift`, `zrow_two_mul_eq_iff_free_tail_eq`, `gateOf`, `SpacedFrom`, `not_spacedFrom_gateOf_rowA_rowB_rowC` |
