| `Rule30SEPToPeriodTwo` | 2026-11-01 | Each of the open hypotheses `OrbitSeparator` (`SEP`), `RW` and `BWH` with `SmallRW` excludes a nonconstant period-two center trace on every nonzero finite row (`sep_implies_period_two`, `rw_implies_period_two`, `bwh_small_implies_period_two`), through a coordinate dictionary between the actual diagram and hard-core streams proved from four local bits (`actual_cone_chart`, `actual_endpoint_hardcore`). For the official center column, each gives that no transient `T` makes `c(t + 2) = c(t)` for all `t ≥ T` (`center_not_eventually_period_two_of_sep`, `..._of_rw`, `..._of_bwh`). Control `finite_origin_control`: finite origin cannot be omitted. None of the hypotheses is proved. `Rule30SeparatorQ01` relates them to `Q01`, which is equivalent to mortality of the auxiliary frontier (`Rule30AlternatingTraceBridge.Q01_of_mortality`, `Rule30FrontierChart.mortality_iff_Q01`): `Q01` implies `SEP` (`Q01_sep`) and `BWH` implies `Q01` (`bwh_Q01`); whether `SEP` or `RW` implies `Q01`, and whether `Q01` implies `RW` or `BWH`, is open. The chart here uses the right half through the hard-core property; the zero-boundary chart of `Rule30SeparatorQ01` uses none. Contributed by David Condrey. Unconditional discharge of `SmallRW` from `BWH` to `OrbitSeparator` and `PeriodTwoExcluded` (`bwh_implies_orbit_separator`, `bwh_implies_period_two`, `center_not_eventually_period_two_of_bwh_unconditional`) contributed by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini ([R30-20261102-f48894](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261102-f48894)). | `sep_implies_period_two`, `rw_implies_period_two`, `bwh_small_implies_period_two`, `finite_origin_control`, `center_not_eventually_period_two_of_sep`, `center_not_eventually_period_two_of_rw`, `center_not_eventually_period_two_of_bwh`, `bwh_implies_orbit_separator`, `bwh_implies_period_two`, `center_not_eventually_period_two_of_bwh_unconditional` |
| `Rule30SeparatorQ01` | 2026-11-08 | Where the open hypotheses `SEP` and `BWH` sit against `Q01`. A chart sends each constrained start of the zero-boundary left half to an endpoint stream whose inverse cone is its own run (`cone_chart`, `peel_chart`), and the endpoint of `Theta f` is hard-core exactly when the free bits `f` have no two adjacent white bits (`hardCore_endOf_iff`). So `ConstantTailSeparator` is `Q01` for those free bits (`sep_iff_QN`, `orbitSeparator_iff_QN`), and `Q01` is `SEP` together with the free bits that meet two adjacent whites infinitely often (`Q01_iff_sep_and`). `Q01` implies `SEP` (`Q01_sep`), and `SEP` implies `Q01Gap` and `CQ01Empty` (`sep_Q01Gap`, `sep_CQ01Empty`). `SEP` is also equivalent to `LR1`: no width-one layer beside the alternating column, with any start cell and any input from column `2`, has a finitely supported forced left row (`sep_iff_LR1`, `layerWord_realizes`). `BWH` is equivalent to exact-width death bounds (`bwh_iff_deathBounds`): every start of exact even width `W ≥ 16` dies by tick `2 W`, and every start of exact odd width `W ≥ 17` with its top two cells black dies by tick `2 W - 2`. So under `BWH` every start supported at depths `≤ W`, `W ≥ 16`, dies by tick `2 W + 4` (`bwh_death`), and `BWH` implies `Q01` (`bwh_Q01`). Kernel-checked controls show that neither guard can be dropped (`odd_guard_needed`, `even_threshold_needed`). None of `SEP`, `BWH` or `Q01` is proved. The chart, `Q01_sep` and `bwh_Q01` reprove results of the external development `lhallee/rule30-open`; its `WedgeLadder.md` already states the guarded exact-width death-time interpretation, formalized here in `bwh_iff_deathBounds`. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261108-752db6](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261108-752db6). | `sep_iff_QN`, `Q01_iff_sep_and`, `Q01_sep`, `sep_Q01Gap`, `sep_iff_LR1`, `cone_chart`, `peel_chart`, `bwh_iff_deathBounds`, `bwh_death`, `bwh_Q01`, `odd_guard_needed`, `even_threshold_needed` |
| `Rule30Rule180LoneSeed` | 2026-11-02 | The Rule 180 orbit of the lone seed. Rule 180 is `a ⊕ b ⊕ bc`, the left-permutive rule `a ⊕ b(1 ⊕ c)`. Its support has a closed form: the single cell `t` at an even time `t`, and the cells `t - 1` and `t` at an odd time `t` (`orbit`, `evolve_seed`), proved by checking one step on that support (`step_even`, `step_odd`). The center column is `1, 1, 0, 0, 0, …` (`center_eq`, `center_black`, `center_white`), and from time `2` on every cell at a position `≤ t - 2` is white (`white_left`). `Rule30WedgeParity.rule180_wedge_odd` gives Rule 180 the same odd wedge counts as Rule 30, so those counts say nothing about the center column. A statement about one rule and one seed; it does not bear on Rule 30. Contributed by David Condrey. | `evolve_seed`, `center_eq`, `center_black`, `center_white`, `white_left` |
| `Rule30TopCoefficient` | 2026-11-02 | The top coefficient of the center function of a quadratic left-permutive rule `a ⊕ g(b, c)`, `g = γ ⊕ α b ⊕ β c ⊕ bc`. With `n = m + 2` and a row on `[-n, n]` given by its cells `c j = x(0, n - j)`, the number of rows with `x(0, -n)` and `x(0, -n + 1)` white and `x(n, 0)` black is odd iff `1 ⊕ α ⊕ β = 1`, for every `γ` (`top_coefficient_rows`). Equivalently the coefficient of `x_{-n+2} ⋯ x_n` in the algebraic normal form of the center function is `1 ⊕ α ⊕ β`: `1` for Rule 30 (`rule30_top_coefficient`) and `0` for Rule 180 (`rule180_top_coefficient`). The proof replaces the boundary diagonal by a triangular change of the summation variables (`psum_tri`), sums out the rightmost cell, which enters the apex affinely (`phi_flip`), and reduces to `Rule30WedgeParity.top_parity_quadratic` (`top_coefficient_quadratic`); the rows are matched to the seed words by a second triangular substitution of the interleaved word (`Y_zdep`, `psum_interleave`). A statement about initial rows of width `2n + 1`, not about the single seed. Contributed by David Condrey. | `top_coefficient_rows`, `top_coefficient_quadratic`, `rule30_top_coefficient`, `rule180_top_coefficient`, `psum_tri` |
| `Rule30CenterCylinders` | 2026-11-09 | An exact cylinder law for the times at which the single-seed center is white and its right neighbour black. For a Rule 30 trajectory `x`, the gap `g_t` is the least `g ∈ {1, …, t}` with `x (t - g) (g - 2) = 1`. For `1 ≤ g ≤ t` and `u = t - g`: `g_t = g`, `x t 0 = 0` and `x t 1 = 1` iff `x u [-g, g + 1] ∈ 1^(2g-1) {010, 011, 100}` (`cylinder_iff`); `g_t = g` and `x t 0 = 0` iff `x u [-g, g - 2] = 1^(2g-1)` and `x u (g - 1) ∨ x u g` (`cylinder_white_iff`). The proof is a backward reconstruction along diagonals: a black cell followed by `g - 1` zero pairs on two adjacent diagonals is a solid interval of `2g - 1` black cells `g` steps earlier (`zero_pairs_iff_solid`). On the single-seed pattern every white center time `t ≥ 2` has a gap with `2 g_t ≤ t` (`gap_exists_of_white`), the gap is unique (`gap_unique`), and on the block `{H + 1, …, 2H}` the number `N11 H` of words `11` in column `1`, which counts the white-center, black-right times, is the sum over `t` and `1 ≤ g ≤ t / 2` of the cylinder indicators, at most one nonzero for each `t` (`white_black_count`). An exact reformulation, not a lower bound: no positive density of the cylinders is claimed. If the center column were eventually `1, 0, 1, 0, …`, the left half-row at a late white time of column `-1` would be a natural number `X` (bit `i` = cell `-1-i`) under the zero-wall map `Rule30ZeroBoundary.IntegerForm.F`, and its wall trace would be a G word at every length: white at time `0`, black at the odd times, no two consecutive white even times (`lang_G`, from `Rule30PeriodTwo.m1_even` and `golden`). | `Rule30PeriodTwoNoFloor` | 2026-11-09 | No density floor for the left half that is the same for every periodic center pattern. The rows are infinite, so they do not contradict `Rule30Coupled.CQ`; the result is a barrier for one kind of argument, a density floor with one constant for every pattern, next to `Rule30ZeroBoundaryBarriers` and the control `Rule30AlternatingTraceBridge.alternatingCenter_without_finiteSupport`. For the coupled question, an open right dichotomy `RD` (every finite right half enters `Rule30GateTrap.trap` at a white time or has eventually periodic column `1` at the white times) with `Rule30GateTrap.Q01Gap` gives `Rule30PeriodTwoCoupled.CQ01` (`CQ01_of_RD_of_Q01Gap`, `no_eventual_period_two_of_RD_of_Q01Gap`). `NoSeedGE2`, `RD`, `Q01Gap` and `Q01` are **open**. Contributed by AIOvernight with Claude (Anthropic) and OpenAI Codex as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261109-62afca](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261109-62afca); closed-form sideways retrodiction of internal zero-wall columns `x X 1..5` under `GValid` (`tr_two_eq_true_of_gvalid`, `x_one_even_odd`, `x_two_even_odd`, `x_three_even_odd`, `x_four_five_zero`), exact pinning of the lowest six seed bits `(false, true, true, false, tr X 4, !tr X 4)` and universal weight invariants `wtH 4 X = 2` and `wtH 6 X = 3` (`seed_bits_zero_to_five`, `wtH_four_and_six_of_gvalid`), and weakening `Q01Gap` to `Q01GapWords` in the `RD` split (`CQ01_of_RD_of_Q01GapWords`, `no_eventual_period_two_of_RD_of_Q01GapWords`), by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-be85cf](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-be85cf). | `lang_GE2`, `certOK_true`, `not_eventually_period_two_of_noSeed_GE2`, `noSeed_GE2_of_Q01`, `not_eventually_period_two_of_Q01_via_E2`, `L_zero_eq_iterate`, `not_eventually_period_two_of_E2_fun`, `not_eventually_period_two_of_E2`, `noSeed_GE2_of_massBoundFun`, `kicks_not_eventually_periodic`, `tr_not_eventually_periodic`, `CQ01_of_RD_of_Q01Gap`, `no_eventual_period_two_of_RD_of_Q01Gap`, `tr_two_eq_true_of_gvalid`, `x_one_even_odd`, `x_two_even_odd`, `x_three_even_of_gvalid`, `x_three_even_odd`, `x_four_five_zero`, `seed_bits_zero_to_five`, `wtH_four_and_six_of_gvalid`, `CQ01_of_RD_of_Q01GapWords`, `no_eventual_period_two_of_RD_of_Q01GapWords` |
| `Rule30ConeTip` | 2026-11-10 | The cone tip of the zero-boundary left half. For every `t ≥ 2` and every row `L`, the second difference of the depth-1 cell `zrow L t 1` in the depths `t - 1` and `t` (`flip`) is `t - 1` modulo `2`, the same on every row (`cone_tip`). A cell reads its deepest input with coefficient `1` (`level_aff`), so flipping depth `d` changes the cells of level `d + 1` by the negated cells of level `d - 1` (`diag`, `chain`), and those flip with depth `d - 1` while the wall stays white (`chain_flip`). For even `t`, every function with vanishing second differences in those directions, every affine function in particular (`affine_second`), misses the tip somewhere on each face of four rows (`face_disagree`), so the tip is not affine (`tip_not_affine`). The mechanism is the left-permutive derivative of `Rule30Equivalents.AlgebraicDegree`, here in the row frame with its white wall. Contributed by Gareth Herbert with Claude (Anthropic) and GPT (OpenAI), as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261110-b07808](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261110-b07808). | `level_aff`, `diag`, `chain_flip`, `cone_tip`, `face_disagree`, `affine_second`, `tip_not_affine` |

## Certificate data

Some theorems rest on finite checks that the kernel evaluates with `decide`. Their data is in the modules under `Rule30Certificates/`:

| Data | For | What it checks |
|---|---|---|
| `White17` | `Rule30White17Runs` | masks, 423 kernel checks (371 transitions via `Rule30FastImage.fcovers`), capture/rank/profile assembly for every block 1^r 0^q, r≥1, q≥17 |
| `Grammar` | `Rule30GrammarRuns` | masks, finite checks and capture/rank/profile assembly for the four-category block grammar |
| `ResetMixtures` | `Rule30ResetMixtures` | width-eight capture, reset and invariant-envelope certificates for arbitrary white gaps |
| `ZeroBoundaryWidth52Chunks0`–`3` | `Rule30ZeroBoundaryWidth52` | Q01 for every start of width at most 52 |
| `EtherTrap0`–`5` | `Rule30EtherTrapWidth20` | that the pure ether lies in no trap of width at most 20 |
| `ZeroBoundaryStripeCycles0`–`3` | `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFamily`, `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFour` | the periodic front-window orbits of the stripe prefixes |
| `StripeTail13Completion` | `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFourComplete` | a punctured lower cycle and parallel return map complete the omitted tail-13 family |
| `WhiteEndChecks` | `Rule30WhiteEnd` | the strip relaxation of `1 0^q` for `q = 10 … 25` |
| `WhitePaddingFamilies`, `FiveTailTube`, `SixTailBackward`, `SixTailEndpoints` | `Rule30ShortTail`, `Rule30FiveTail`, `Rule30SixTail`, `Rule30SixTailSharpened`, `Rule30SuffixFamilies` | the pumping, vacuum-avoidance and backward-tube certificates of the tails of at most six cells |
| `StripeMeeting` | `Rule30StripeMeeting` | a transducer of 2321 states for stripe relations |
| `GateTrap` | `Rule30GateTrap` | a closed trap of 829 sixteen-cell windows for the right half driven by the alternating column, its four gap layers, and the window of the empty right half at time 293 |
| `PeriodTwoMass` | `Rule30PeriodTwoMass` | Conjecture S for every length `H ≤ 90`: an exhaustive search over the seeds of the zero-wall map, with the seed weight carried along and both children's trace bits from one iteration (`fcert90`) |
| `PeriodTwoWidth13` | `Rule30PeriodTwoWidth13` | a rank certificate and gap-automaton labels on the `16384` nodes of the width-13 strip beside an alternating center, column `14` free, in `16` chunks of `1024` nodes |

The catalog takes certificate data only as a step in a theorem about an infinite family: all words of a shape, all widths, all phases, every configuration with a leftmost black cell. A list of words, widths, windows or depths for its own sake is not taken; it stays on the submissions site. A contribution uses an existing checker where one fits (the rank certificates of `Rule30StripCertificates`, the strips of `Rule30FiniteStrips`, the backward tubes of `Rule30BackwardTube`); a new checker comes with its soundness theorem and generalizes or replaces one. Its data goes in one module per family, which builds in under a minute and 4 GB; the heavier modules above are listed with their cost under [Building](#building). For padded words `0^r u`, a lower bound or a longer tail is a change to `Rule30PaddedWords`, not a new module.

## Which modules predate the attempt

The birth times of the catalog files (`stat -f %B`) and the docstrings agree. The *Added* column of the tables below uses these labels.

| Added | When | Modules |
|---|---|---|
| `10-10` | 2026-10-10 | Five catalog entries from Wolfram's writings, written before the attempt: `Rule30Predecessors`, `Rule30PredecessorCounts`, `BusyBeaverLifetimes`, `Rule30Blocks`, `Rule30Jen`. Their docstrings cite only Wolfram and the literature. |
| `start` | 2026-10-15, 04:07–04:16 | The first Rule 30 Prize modules, written before the multi-agent rounds: `Rule30Prize`, `Rule30Diagonals`, `Rule30Window`. Their docstrings do not cite the multi-agent project. |
| `R1`, `R2`, `final` | 2026-10-15, 07:18, 09:40, 11:49 | The other 46 modules: round 1 (11), round 2 (7) and the final batch (28, including the 13 in `Rule30Certificates/`). Their docstrings cite the Rule 30 Prize project (2026). |

## Modules by topic

All modules live in `Ruliology/CellularAutomata/`. Theorems live in the namespace `Ruliology.CellularAutomata.<Module>`, with these exceptions:

- `Rule30Predecessors` and `Rule30PredecessorCounts` use `…Rule30`.
- The six `Rule30EtherThree*` modules of the snapshot share `…Rule30EtherThree`; `Rule30EtherThreeErosion` uses its own.
- `Rule30EtherTrapWidth20` uses `…Rule30EtherTrap`.
- `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFamily` and `Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFour` use `…Rule30ZeroBoundaryStripeFront`.
- `Rule30RightWallLocks` and `Rule30RightWallCloud` use `…Rule30RightWall`.
- The data modules in `Rule30Certificates/` use namespaces inside the module they serve, for example `…Rule30ZeroBoundaryWidth52.C0`.

"Open" marks a statement defined as a `Prop` but not proved.

### Period 1

| Module | Added | What it proves | Headline theorems |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rule30Prize` | start | States Problems 1 and 2 as in formal-conjectures' `Other/Rule30.lean`. No column is eventually constant, so period 1 is excluded for every column and every transient. | `Problem1`, `Problem2`, `no_column_eventually_constant`, `center_not_eventually_period_one` |

| Module | Added | What it proves | Headline theorems |
|---|---|---|---|

| `Rule30FactorWindow` | R1 | Any eventual period P from time T has T + P ≥ 16359, because the first 16,384 bits have 16,359 distinct factors of length 26. | `transient_add_period_ge` |

### Barriers

| Module | Added | What it proves | Headline theorems |

| `Rule30Realization` | R1 | Some configuration realizes any right half together with any column 0. Rules 90, 150 and 210 meet Jen's hypotheses, yet their center columns are eventually constant. | `column_realizable_unique`, `seed_perturbation`, `barrier` |
| `Rule30ZeroBoundaryBarriers` | R2 | For `Q01` there is no ranking through a finite abstraction, no local ranking and no edge-DFA ranking. Also gives a two-sided barrier. | `finite_abstraction_has_lasso`, `no_local_ranking`, `no_edge_dfa_ranking`, `no_joint_local_ranking` |
| `Rule30EtherWallWitness` | 2026-10-31 | A periodic left half cannot replace the finite one in `Rule30Ether.lock_excludes` and `absorption_excludes`: for `0001`, `0011`, `0111` and the alternating column, a left half of spatial period 7 (the ether) and a finite right half (ether columns, a wall state, then white) give center column `w^∞` from time 0. | `period_four_left_periodic_right_finite`, `alt_left_periodic_right_finite`, `alt_coupled_left_periodic` |

### Other catalog entries (not about Problem 1)

| Module | Added | What it proves | Headline theorems |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rule30Predecessors` | 10-10 | A corrected form of the Feynmans' predecessor theorem: rule 30 on a cyclic register. | `predCount_eq_three_iff`, `predCount_eq_one_iff` |
| `Rule30EtherGrounding` | 2026-10-31 | A backward-closed temporal pair invariant with arbitrary odd-time bits cannot reach a constant black edge. Thus no two adjacent co-moving columns can eventually follow the one-in-four ether, uniformly over all initial codes, source depths, spatial/time phases and onset times, without any global period assumption. Includes the actual-row right-edge conjugacy and a positive control for the two-sided ether. Does not prove alternating-tail exclusion, general same-edge rigidity or Problem 1. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex and Claude (Anthropic); exact `d`-step phase descent (`pair_descends_steps`, `pair_reaches_edge_exact`), finite 8-tick window exclusion at the black edge (`no_eight_tick_pair_at_edge`), the 1-in-4 even-time law (`ether_even_time_one_in_four`), and diagonal shift symmetry (`ether_shift_one_two`, `ether_shift_mul`) by G.P.J.W. Quint and Gemini as Rule 30 Prize submission [R30-20261104-776689](https://www.wolframcloud.com/obj/rule-30-prize/Agents/status/R30-20261104-776689). | `pair_descends`, `no_eventual_ether_pair`, `no_eventual_ether_tail`, `no_moving_ether_pair`, `ether_step`, `good_mod_eight`, `hasPair_mod_eight_iff`, `pair_descends_steps`, `pair_reaches_edge_exact`, `no_eight_tick_pair_at_edge`, `ether_col_period_four`, `ether_time_period_eight`, `ether_even_time_one_in_four`, `ether_shift_one_two`, `ether_shift_mul` |
| `Rule30GroupCocycle` | 2026-10-30 | For any group and independently placed finite row windows, a homogeneous one-step zero-curvature connection is a local vertex gauge of two commuting vacuum constants; cyclic spatial products are conjugate to vacuum powers. No finiteness, commutativity, identity-vacuum, or minimal-range premise. Includes pure-gauge controls and a Boolean local-flux corollary; no prize solution or noninvertible/nonlocal classification. Contributed by Troy Lowry with OpenAI Codex. | `arbitrary_local_supports`, `full_background_connection`, `cyclic_products_background`, `boolean_flux_coboundary` |
| `Rule30Problem3Bounds` | 2026-10-31 | Head-cone prefix locality, 2-adic initial-tape congruence, transient elimination, and unconditional step lower bounds for one-tape Turing machines (`Rule30Problem3.Machine`) computing the center column from LSB-first binary digits. In `t` steps from `head = 0`, `M.run n t` reads only cells `0 ≤ i < t` (`run_eq_of_init_eq_lt`). For `k ≥ 1`, all inputs `m, n ≥ 2^(k-1)` with `m ≡ n (mod 2^k)` share identical initial tapes on `i < k` (`init_tape_eq_of_mod_two_pow_eq`), so halting in `≤ k` steps on even a single input `n ≥ 2^(k-1)` forces `f` to be constant across `{m ≥ 2^(k-1) : m ≡ n (mod 2^k)}` (`computes_eq_of_mod_two_pow_of_run_le`, `computes_const_on_dyadic_ray`). A single residue-class color flip forces `k + 1 ≤ T n` across that residue class (`time_gt_of_mod_two_pow_ne`) and `Nat.log2 n + 2 ≤ T n` when flipping above the MSB (`log2_succ_succ_le_time_of_ray_flip`), while eventual time `T n ≤ k` from `N` on forces period `2^k` from the fixed transient `2^(k-1)` independent of `N` (`period_of_eventually_time_le`, `evPer_two_pow_of_eventually_time_le`, `time_unbounded_of_problem1`), which with `Rule30FactorWindow.transient_add_period_ge` implies unconditionally (without `Problem1`) that every machine computing `center` takes `≥ k + 1` steps infinitely often for every `k ≤ 13` (`time_unbounded_le_thirteen`, up to `14` steps), and with `Rule30LeftUnbounded.D_period_unbounded` gives left diagonals requiring `≥ k + 1` steps infinitely often (`diagonal_time_unbounded`). In fact, `T r ≤ k` on the canonical window `[2^(k-1), 2^(k-1) + 2^k)` of `2^k` inputs already forces period `2^k` from `2^(k-1)` on (`period_of_canon_window_time_le`), so for every `k` some left diagonal `D j` (`j ≤ 2^(2*2^k)`) requires `≥ k + 1` steps on `[2^(k-1), 2^(k-1) + 2^k)` itself (`diagonal_canon_window_time_ge`). Evaluating the minimal residue-flip witnesses for `k = 1..4` (`t ≤ 99`) via `fastCenter` (`center_eq_fastCenter`) proves unconditionally that every machine computing `center` satisfies `k + 1 ≤ T n` for `n ≥ 2^(k-1)` (`1 ≤ k ≤ 4`) and `Nat.log2 n + 2 ≤ T n` for `1 ≤ n < 16`. Contributed by G.P.J.W.
